```
1→"""Ranking and grouping helpers for work queue selection."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.helpers import (
     6→    detail_dict,
     7→    is_review_finding,
     8→    is_subjective_finding,
     9→    primary_command_for_finding,
    10→    review_finding_weight,
    11→    scope_matches,
    12→    slugify,
    13→    status_matches,
    14→    subjective_strict_scores,
    15→    supported_fixers_for_item,
    16→)
    17→from desloppify.engine.planning.common import CONFIDENCE_ORDER
    18→from desloppify.state import path_scoped_findings
    19→
    20→
    21→def subjective_score_value(item: dict) -> float:
    22→    if item.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension":
    23→        detail = detail_dict(item)
    24→        return float(detail.get("strict_score", item.get("subjective_score", 100.0)))
    25→    return float(item.get("subjective_score", 100.0))
    26→
    27→
    28→def build_finding_items(
    29→    state: dict,
    30→    *,
    31→    scan_path: str | None,
    32→    status_filter: str,
    33→    scope: str | None,
    34→    chronic: bool,
    35→) -> list[dict]:
    36→    scoped = path_scoped_findings(state.get("findings", {}), scan_path)
    37→    subjective_scores = subjective_strict_scores(state)
    38→    out: list[dict] = []
    39→
    40→    for finding_id, finding in scoped.items():
    41→        if finding.get("suppressed"):
    42→            continue
    43→        if not status_matches(finding.get("status", "open"), status_filter):
    44→            continue
    45→        if chronic and not (
    46→            finding.get("status") == "open" and finding.get("reopen_count", 0) >= 2
    47→        ):
    48→            continue
    49→
    50→        item = dict(finding)
    51→        item["id"] = finding_id
    52→        item["kind"] = "finding"
    53→        item["is_review"] = is_review_finding(item)
    54→        item["is_subjective"] = is_subjective_finding(item)
    55→        item["effective_tier"] = (
    56→            4 if item["is_subjective"] else int(finding.get("tier", 3))
    57→        )
    58→        item["review_weight"] = (
    59→            review_finding_weight(item) if item["is_review"] else None
    60→        )
    61→        subjective_score = None
    62→        if item["is_subjective"]:
    63→            detail = detail_dict(finding)
    64→            dim_name = detail.get("dimension_name", "")
    65→            dim_key = detail.get("dimension", "") or slugify(dim_name)
    66→            subjective_score = subjective_scores.get(
    67→                dim_key, subjective_scores.get(dim_name.lower(), 100.0)
    68→            )
    69→        item["subjective_score"] = subjective_score
    70→        supported_fixers = supported_fixers_for_item(state, item)
    71→        item["primary_command"] = primary_command_for_finding(
    72→            item,
    73→            supported_fixers=supported_fixers,
    74→        )
    75→
    76→        if not scope_matches(item, scope):
    77→            continue
    78→        out.append(item)
    79→
    80→    return out
    81→
    82→
    83→def item_sort_key(item: dict) -> tuple:
    84→    if item.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension" or item.get("is_subjective"):
    85→        return (
    86→            int(item.get("effective_tier", 4)),
    87→            1,  # Subjective items sort after mechanical items within T4.
    88→            subjective_score_value(item),
    89→            item.get("id", ""),
    90→        )
    91→
    92→    detail = detail_dict(item)
    93→    review_weight = float(item.get("review_weight", 0.0) or 0.0)
    94→    return (
    95→        int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3))),
    96→        0,
    97→        CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(item.get("confidence", "low"), 9),
    98→        -review_weight,
    99→        -int(detail.get("count", 0) or 0),
   100→        item.get("id", ""),
   101→    )
   102→
   103→
   104→def item_explain(item: dict) -> dict:
   105→    effective_tier = int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3)))
   106→
   107→    if item.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension":
   108→        return {
   109→            "kind": "subjective_dimension",
   110→            "effective_tier": effective_tier,
   111→            "subjective_score": subjective_score_value(item),
   112→            "policy": (
   113→                "Subjective dimensions are always queued as T4 and do not outrank "
   114→                "mechanical T1/T2/T3 items."
   115→            ),
   116→            "ranking_factors": ["tier asc", "subjective_score asc", "id asc"],
   117→        }
   118→
   119→    detail = detail_dict(item)
   120→    confidence = item.get("confidence", "low")
   121→    is_subjective = bool(item.get("is_subjective"))
   122→    is_review = bool(item.get("is_review"))
   123→    ranking_factors: list[str]
   124→    if is_subjective:
   125→        ranking_factors = ["tier fixed to T4", "subjective_score asc", "id asc"]
   126→    elif is_review:
   127→        ranking_factors = [
   128→            "tier asc",
   129→            "confidence asc",
   130→            "review_weight desc",
   131→            "count desc",
   132→            "id asc",
   133→        ]
   134→    else:
   135→        ranking_factors = ["tier asc", "confidence asc", "count desc", "id asc"]
   136→    explain = {
   137→        "kind": "finding",
   138→        "effective_tier": effective_tier,
   139→        "confidence": confidence,
   140→        "confidence_rank": CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(confidence, 9),
   141→        "count": int(detail.get("count", 0) or 0),
   142→        "id": item.get("id", ""),
   143→        "ranking_factors": ranking_factors,
   144→    }
   145→    if is_review:
   146→        explain["review_weight"] = float(item.get("review_weight", 0.0) or 0.0)
   147→    if is_subjective:
   148→        explain["policy"] = (
   149→            "Subjective findings are forced to T4 and do not outrank "
   150→            "mechanical T1/T2/T3 items."
   151→        )
   152→        explain["subjective_score"] = subjective_score_value(item)
   153→    return explain
   154→
   155→
   156→def tier_counts(items: list[dict]) -> dict[int, int]:
   157→    counts = {1: 0, 2: 0, 3: 0, 4: 0}
   158→    for item in items:
   159→        tier = int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3)))
   160→        counts[tier] = counts.get(tier, 0) + 1
   161→    return counts
   162→
   163→
   164→def choose_fallback_tier(requested_tier: int, counts: dict[int, int]) -> int | None:
   165→    available = [tier for tier, count in counts.items() if count > 0]
   166→    if not available:
   167→        return None
   168→    return min(available, key=lambda tier: (abs(tier - requested_tier), tier))
   169→
   170→
   171→def group_queue_items(items: list[dict], group: str) -> dict[str, list[dict]]:
   172→    """Group queue items for alternate output modes."""
   173→    grouped: dict[str, list[dict]] = {}
   174→    for item in items:
   175→        if group == "file":
   176→            key = item.get("file", "")
   177→        elif group == "detector":
   178→            key = item.get("detector", "")
   179→        elif group == "tier":
   180→            key = f"T{int(item.get('effective_tier', item.get('tier', 3)))}"
   181→        elif group == "cluster":
   182→            plan_cluster = item.get("plan_cluster")
   183→            key = plan_cluster["name"] if isinstance(plan_cluster, dict) else "(unclustered)"
   184→        else:
   185→            key = "items"
   186→        grouped.setdefault(key, []).append(item)
   187→    return grouped
   188→
   189→
   190→__all__ = [
   191→    "build_finding_items",
   192→    "choose_fallback_tier",
   193→    "item_explain",
   194→    "item_sort_key",
   195→    "tier_counts",
   196→    "subjective_score_value",
   197→    "group_queue_items",
   198→]
   199→
```

```
1→"""Dimension projection policy for scorecard rendering."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.app.output.scorecard_parts.dimension_policy import (
     6→    _DEFAULT_ELEGANCE_COMPONENTS,
     7→    _ELEGANCE_COMPONENTS_BY_LANG,
     8→    _SUBJECTIVE_SCORECARD_ORDER_BY_LANG,
     9→    _SUBJECTIVE_SCORECARD_ORDER_DEFAULT,
    10→)
    11→from desloppify.app.output.scorecard_parts.dimension_policy import (
    12→    _SCORECARD_MAX_DIMENSIONS as SCORECARD_MAX_DIMENSIONS,
    13→)
    14→
    15→
    16→def resolve_scorecard_lang(state: dict) -> str | None:
    17→    """Best-effort current scan language key for scorecard display policy."""
    18→    history = state.get("scan_history")
    19→    if isinstance(history, list):
    20→        for entry in reversed(history):
    21→            if not isinstance(entry, dict):
    22→                continue
    23→            lang = entry.get("lang")
    24→            if isinstance(lang, str) and lang.strip():
    25→                return lang.strip().lower()
    26→
    27→    capabilities = state.get("lang_capabilities")
    28→    if isinstance(capabilities, dict) and len(capabilities) == 1:
    29→        only_lang = next(iter(capabilities.keys()))
    30→        if isinstance(only_lang, str) and only_lang.strip():
    31→            return only_lang.strip().lower()
    32→
    33→    findings = state.get("findings")
    34→    if isinstance(findings, dict):
    35→        counts: dict[str, int] = {}
    36→        for finding in findings.values():
    37→            if not isinstance(finding, dict):
    38→                continue
    39→            lang = finding.get("lang")
    40→            if isinstance(lang, str) and lang.strip():
    41→                key = lang.strip().lower()
    42→                counts[key] = counts.get(key, 0) + 1
    43→        if counts:
    44→            return max(counts, key=counts.get)
    45→    return None
    46→
    47→
    48→def is_unassessed_subjective_placeholder(data: dict) -> bool:
    49→    return (
    50→        "subjective_assessment" in data.get("detectors", {})
    51→        and data.get("score", 0) == 0
    52→        and data.get("issues", 0) == 0
    53→    )
    54→
    55→
    56→def collapse_elegance_dimensions(
    57→    active_dims: list[tuple[str, dict]],
    58→    *,
    59→    lang_key: str | None,
    60→) -> list[tuple[str, dict]]:
    61→    """Collapse High/Mid/Low elegance rows into one aggregate display row."""
    62→    component_names = set(
    63→        _ELEGANCE_COMPONENTS_BY_LANG.get(lang_key or "", _DEFAULT_ELEGANCE_COMPONENTS)
    64→    )
    65→    elegance_rows = [
    66→        (name, data) for name, data in active_dims if name in component_names
    67→    ]
    68→    if not elegance_rows:
    69→        return active_dims
    70→
    71→    remaining_rows = [
    72→        (name, data) for name, data in active_dims if name not in component_names
    73→    ]
    74→    count = len(elegance_rows)
    75→    score_avg = round(
    76→        sum(float(data.get("score", 100.0)) for _, data in elegance_rows) / count, 1
    77→    )
    78→    strict_avg = round(
    79→        sum(
    80→            float(data.get("strict", data.get("score", 100.0)))
    81→            for _, data in elegance_rows
    82→        )
    83→        / count,
    84→        1,
    85→    )
    86→    checks_total = sum(int(data.get("checks", 0)) for _, data in elegance_rows)
    87→    issues_total = sum(int(data.get("issues", 0)) for _, data in elegance_rows)
    88→    tier = max(int(data.get("tier", 4)) for _, data in elegance_rows)
    89→    placeholder_flags = [
    90→        bool(
    91→            data.get("detectors", {})
    92→            .get("subjective_assessment", {})
    93→            .get("placeholder")
    94→        )
    95→        for _, data in elegance_rows
    96→    ]
    97→
    98→    label = "Elegance"
    99→    if any(name.lower() == label.lower() for name, _ in remaining_rows):
   100→        label = "Elegance (combined)"
   101→
   102→    pass_rate = round(score_avg / 100.0, 4)
   103→    return [
   104→        *remaining_rows,
   105→        (
   106→            label,
   107→            {
   108→                "score": score_avg,
   109→                "strict": strict_avg,
   110→                "checks": checks_total,
   111→                "issues": issues_total,
   112→                "tier": tier,
   113→                "detectors": {
   114→                    "subjective_assessment": {
   115→                        "potential": checks_total,
   116→                        "pass_rate": pass_rate,
   117→                        "issues": issues_total,
   118→                        "weighted_failures": round(checks_total * (1 - pass_rate), 4),
   119→                        "components": [name for name, _ in elegance_rows],
   120→                        "placeholder": any(placeholder_flags),
   121→                    }
   122→                },
   123→            },
   124→        ),
   125→    ]
   126→
   127→
   128→def limit_scorecard_dimensions(
   129→    active_dims: list[tuple[str, dict]],
   130→    *,
   131→    lang_key: str | None,
   132→    max_rows: int = SCORECARD_MAX_DIMENSIONS,
   133→) -> list[tuple[str, dict]]:
   134→    """Limit scorecard rows with language-specific subjective priority."""
   135→    if len(active_dims) <= max_rows:
   136→        return active_dims
   137→
   138→    mechanical = [
   139→        (name, data)
   140→        for name, data in active_dims
   141→        if "subjective_assessment" not in data.get("detectors", {})
   142→    ]
   143→    subjective = [
   144→        (name, data)
   145→        for name, data in active_dims
   146→        if "subjective_assessment" in data.get("detectors", {})
   147→    ]
   148→    if len(mechanical) >= max_rows:
   149→        return mechanical[:max_rows]
   150→
   151→    budget = max_rows - len(mechanical)
   152→    preferred_order = _SUBJECTIVE_SCORECARD_ORDER_BY_LANG.get(
   153→        lang_key or "",
   154→        _SUBJECTIVE_SCORECARD_ORDER_DEFAULT,
   155→    )
   156→
   157→    remaining = {name: (name, data) for name, data in subjective}
   158→    selected: list[tuple[str, dict]] = []
   159→    for name in preferred_order:
   160→        row = remaining.pop(name, None)
   161→        if row is None:
   162→            continue
   163→        selected.append(row)
   164→        if len(selected) >= budget:
   165→            break
   166→
   167→    if len(selected) < budget and remaining:
   168→        extras = sorted(
   169→            remaining.values(),
   170→            key=lambda item: (
   171→                float(item[1].get("strict", item[1].get("score", 100.0))),
   172→                item[0],
   173→            ),
   174→        )
   175→        selected.extend(extras[: budget - len(selected)])
   176→
   177→    return [*mechanical, *selected]
   178→
   179→
   180→def prepare_scorecard_dimensions(state: dict) -> list[tuple[str, dict]]:
   181→    """Prepare scorecard rows (active, elegance-collapsed, capped).
   182→
   183→    Dimensions are derived dynamically from what the scoring engine produced
   184→    in ``dimension_scores`` — no per-language hard-coded dimension list.
   185→    Unassessed subjective placeholders (score 0, no issues) are excluded so
   186→    the scorecard only shows dimensions that have real data.
   187→    """
   188→    dim_scores = state.get("dimension_scores", {})
   189→    if not isinstance(dim_scores, dict):
   190→        return []
   191→
   192→    all_dims = [
   193→        (name, data) for name, data in dim_scores.items() if isinstance(data, dict)
   194→    ]
   195→
   196→    [REDACTED](state)
   197→    all_dims = collapse_elegance_dimensions(all_dims, lang_key=lang_key)
   198→
   199→    # Show dimensions with real data; skip unassessed subjective placeholders.
   200→    active_dims = [
   201→        (name, data)
   202→        for name, data in all_dims
   203→        if data.get("checks", 0) > 0 and not is_unassessed_subjective_placeholder(data)
   204→    ]
   205→    active_dims = limit_scorecard_dimensions(active_dims, lang_key=lang_key)
   206→    active_dims.sort(key=lambda x: (0 if x[0] == "File health" else 1, x[0]))
   207→    return active_dims
   208→
   209→
   210→__all__ = [
   211→    "SCORECARD_MAX_DIMENSIONS",
   212→    "collapse_elegance_dimensions",
   213→    "prepare_scorecard_dimensions",
   214→    "limit_scorecard_dimensions",
   215→    "resolve_scorecard_lang",
   216→]
   217→
```

```
1→"""Helper utilities for work queue item construction."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import re
     6→from fnmatch import fnmatch
     7→
     8→from desloppify.core.enums import finding_status_tokens
     9→from desloppify.core.registry import DETECTORS
    10→from desloppify.engine.planning.scorecard_projection import (
    11→    scorecard_subjective_entries,
    12→)
    13→from desloppify.intelligence.integrity import (
    14→    is_holistic_subjective_finding,
    15→    unassessed_subjective_dimensions,
    16→)
    17→from desloppify.scoring import DISPLAY_NAMES
    18→
    19→ALL_STATUSES = set(finding_status_tokens(include_all=True))
    20→ATTEST_EXAMPLE = (
    21→    "I have actually [DESCRIBE THE CONCRETE CHANGE YOU MADE] "
    22→    "and I am not gaming the score by resolving without fixing."
    23→)
    24→
    25→
    26→def detail_dict(item: dict) -> dict:
    27→    """Return finding detail as a dict; tolerate legacy/non-dict payloads."""
    28→    detail = item.get("detail")
    29→    return detail if isinstance(detail, dict) else {}
    30→
    31→
    32→def status_matches(item_status: str, status_filter: str) -> bool:
    33→    return status_filter == "all" or item_status == status_filter
    34→
    35→
    36→def is_subjective_finding(item: dict) -> bool:
    37→    detector = item.get("detector")
    38→    if detector in {"subjective_assessment"}:
    39→        return True
    40→    if detector == "holistic_review":
    41→        return True
    42→    return False
    43→
    44→
    45→def is_review_finding(item: dict) -> bool:
    46→    return item.get("detector") == "review"
    47→
    48→
    49→def review_finding_weight(item: dict) -> float:
    50→    """Return review issue weight aligned with issues list ordering."""
    51→    confidence = str(item.get("confidence", "low")).lower()
    52→    weight_by_confidence = {
    53→        "high": 1.0,
    54→        "medium": 0.7,
    55→        "low": 0.3,
    56→    }
    57→    weight = weight_by_confidence.get(confidence, 0.3)
    58→    if detail_dict(item).get("holistic"):
    59→        weight *= 10.0
    60→    return float(weight)
    61→
    62→
    63→def scope_matches(item: dict, scope: str | None) -> bool:
    64→    """Apply show-style pattern matching against a queue item."""
    65→    if not scope:
    66→        return True
    67→
    68→    item_id = item.get("id", "")
    69→    detector = item.get("detector", "")
    70→    filepath = item.get("file", "")
    71→    summary = item.get("summary", "")
    72→    dimension = detail_dict(item).get("dimension_name", "")
    73→    kind = item.get("kind", "")
    74→
    75→    if "*" in scope:
    76→        return any(
    77→            fnmatch(candidate, scope)
    78→            for candidate in (item_id, filepath, detector, dimension, summary)
    79→        )
    80→
    81→    if "::" in scope:
    82→        return item_id.startswith(scope)
    83→
    84→    lowered = scope.lower()
    85→    if kind == "subjective_dimension":
    86→        return (
    87→            lowered in item_id.lower()
    88→            or lowered in dimension.lower()
    89→            or lowered in summary.lower()
    90→        )
    91→
    92→    # Hash suffix: 8+ hex chars matches the tail segment of a finding ID.
    93→    if len(lowered) >= 8 and re.fullmatch(r"[0-9a-f]+", lowered):
    94→        return item_id.lower().endswith("::" + lowered)
    95→
    96→    return (
    97→        detector == scope
    98→        or filepath == scope
    99→        or filepath.startswith(scope.rstrip("/") + "/")
   100→    )
   101→
   102→
   103→def slugify(text: str) -> str:
   104→    return re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9_]+", "_", text.lower()).strip("_")
   105→
   106→
   107→def _canonical_subjective_dimension_key(display_name: str) -> str:
   108→    """Map a display label (e.g. 'Mid elegance') to its canonical dimension key."""
   109→    cleaned = display_name.replace(" (subjective)", "").strip()
   110→    target = cleaned.lower()
   111→
   112→    for dim_key, label in DISPLAY_NAMES.items():
   113→        if str(label).lower() == target:
   114→            return str(dim_key)
   115→    return slugify(cleaned)
   116→
   117→
   118→def _subjective_dimension_aliases(display_name: str) -> set[str]:
   119→    """Return normalized aliases used to match display labels with finding dimension keys."""
   120→    cleaned = display_name.replace(" (subjective)", "").strip()
   121→    canonical = _canonical_subjective_dimension_key(cleaned)
   122→    return {
   123→        cleaned.lower(),
   124→        cleaned.replace(" ", "_").lower(),
   125→        slugify(cleaned),
   126→        canonical.lower(),
   127→        slugify(canonical),
   128→    }
   129→
   130→
   131→def supported_fixers_for_item(state: dict, item: dict) -> set[str] | None:
   132→    """Return supported fixers for an item's language when known."""
   133→    lang = str(item.get("lang", "") or "").strip()
   134→    if not lang:
   135→        return None
   136→
   137→    caps = state.get("lang_capabilities", {})
   138→    if not isinstance(caps, dict):
   139→        return None
   140→
   141→    lang_caps = caps.get(lang, {})
   142→    if not isinstance(lang_caps, dict):
   143→        return None
   144→
   145→    fixers = lang_caps.get("fixers")
   146→    if not isinstance(fixers, list):
   147→        return None
   148→    return {fixer for fixer in fixers if isinstance(fixer, str)}
   149→
   150→
```

```
1→"""Lightweight integrity helpers for subjective/review scoring.
     2→
     3→This module intentionally lives outside ``desloppify.intelligence.review`` so command/state
     4→paths can import it without loading the heavier review package.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→from collections.abc import Iterable, Mapping
    10→
    11→from desloppify.scoring import (
    12→    SUBJECTIVE_TARGET_MATCH_TOLERANCE,
    13→    matches_target_score,
    14→)
    15→
    16→__all__ = [
    17→    "SUBJECTIVE_TARGET_MATCH_TOLERANCE",
    18→    "is_holistic_subjective_finding",
    19→    "is_subjective_review_open",
    20→    "matches_target_score",
    21→    "subjective_review_open_breakdown",
    22→    "unassessed_subjective_dimensions",
    23→]
    24→
    25→
    26→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    27→# Internal iteration helper
    28→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    29→
    30→
    31→def _iter_findings(
    32→    findings: Mapping[str, dict] | Iterable[dict],
    33→) -> Iterable[tuple[str, dict]]:
    34→    """Yield (finding_id, finding) pairs from mapping or iterable inputs."""
    35→    if isinstance(findings, Mapping):
    36→        for finding_id, finding in findings.items():
    37→            if isinstance(finding, dict):
    38→                yield str(finding_id), finding
    39→        return
    40→
    41→    for index, finding in enumerate(findings):
    42→        if isinstance(finding, dict):
    43→            yield str(index), finding
    44→
    45→
    46→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    47→# Public helpers (formerly in integrity/review.py)
    48→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    49→
    50→
    51→def is_subjective_review_open(finding: dict) -> bool:
    52→    """Return True when a finding is an open subjective-review signal."""
    53→    return (
    54→        finding.get("status") == "open"
    55→        and finding.get("detector") == "subjective_review"
    56→    )
    57→
    58→
    59→def is_holistic_subjective_finding(finding: dict, *, finding_id: str = "") -> bool:
    60→    """Best-effort check for holistic subjective-review coverage findings."""
    61→    candidate_id = str(finding.get("id") or finding_id or "")
    62→    if "::holistic_unreviewed" in candidate_id or "::holistic_stale" in candidate_id:
    63→        return True
    64→
    65→    summary = str(finding.get("summary", "") or "").lower()
    66→    if "holistic" in summary and "review" in summary:
    67→        return True
    68→
    69→    detail = finding.get("detail", {})
    70→    return bool(detail.get("holistic"))
    71→
    72→
    73→def subjective_review_open_breakdown(
    74→    findings: Mapping[str, dict] | Iterable[dict],
    75→) -> tuple[int, dict[str, int], dict[str, int]]:
    76→    """Return open subjective count plus reason and holistic-reason breakdowns."""
    77→    reason_counts: dict[str, int] = {}
    78→    holistic_reason_counts: dict[str, int] = {}
    79→    total = 0
    80→
    81→    for finding_id, finding in _iter_findings(findings):
    82→        if not is_subjective_review_open(finding):
    83→            continue
    84→
    85→        total += 1
    86→        reason = str(finding.get("detail", {}).get("reason", "other") or "other")
    87→        reason_counts[reason] = reason_counts.get(reason, 0) + 1
    88→
    89→        if is_holistic_subjective_finding(finding, finding_id=finding_id):
    90→            holistic_reason_counts[reason] = holistic_reason_counts.get(reason, 0) + 1
    91→
    92→    return total, reason_counts, holistic_reason_counts
    93→
    94→
    95→def unassessed_subjective_dimensions(dim_scores: dict | None) -> list[str]:
    96→    """Return subjective dimension display names that are still 0% placeholders."""
    97→    if not dim_scores:
    98→        return []
    99→
   100→    unassessed: list[str] = []
```

```
    49→    "global_mutable_config": DetectorScoringPolicy(

    51→    ),
    52→    "orphaned": DetectorScoringPolicy("orphaned", "Code quality", 3),

    68→        "boilerplate_duplication", "Duplication", 3
    69→    ),
    70→    # Test health
    71→    "test_coverage": DetectorScoringPolicy(
    72→        "test_coverage", "Test health", 4, file_based=True, use_loc_weight=True
    73→    ),
    74→    "subjective_review": DetectorScoringPolicy(
    75→        "subjective_review", "Test health", 4, file_based=True
    76→    ),
    77→    # Security
    78→    "security": DetectorScoringPolicy(
    79→        "security",
    80→        "Security",
    81→        4,
    82→        file_based=True,
    83→        excluded_zones=SECURITY_EXCLUDED_ZONES,
    84→    ),
    85→    "cycles": DetectorScoringPolicy("cycles", "Security", 4),
    86→    # Design coherence (concerns confirmed by subjective review)
    87→    "concerns": DetectorScoringPolicy("concerns", None, None, file_based=True),
    88→    # Review findings are scored via subjective dimensions, not mechanical dimensions.
    89→    "review": DetectorScoringPolicy("review", None, None, file_based=True),
    90→}
    91→
    92→# Detectors where potential = file count but findings are per-(file, sub-type).
    93→# Per-file weighted failures are capped at 1.0 to match the file-based denominator.
    94→FILE_BASED_DETECTORS = {
    95→    detector
    96→    for detector, policy in DETECTOR_SCORING_POLICIES.items()
    97→    if policy.file_based
    98→}
    99→
   100→
   101→def _build_dimensions() -> list[Dimension]:
   102→    """Derive dimensions from DETECTOR_SCORING_POLICIES.
   103→
   104→    Each unique (dimension, tier) pair becomes a Dimension, with its detectors
   105→    collected automatically. Order follows first-seen in the policies dict.
   106→    """
   107→    # Collect (dimension_name -> tier) preserving first-seen order,
   108→    # and group detectors by dimension.
   109→    dim_tiers: dict[str, int] = {}
   110→    grouped: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
   111→    for detector, policy in DETECTOR_SCORING_POLICIES.items():
   112→        if policy.dimension is None or policy.tier is None:
   113→            continue
   114→        if policy.dimension not in dim_tiers:
   115→            dim_tiers[policy.dimension] = policy.tier
   116→            grouped[policy.dimension] = []
   117→        grouped[policy.dimension].append(detector)
   118→    return [
   119→        Dimension(name=name, tier=tier, detectors=grouped[name])
   120→        for name, tier in dim_tiers.items()
   121→    ]
   122→
   123→
   124→DIMENSIONS = _build_dimensions()
   125→DIMENSIONS_BY_NAME = {d.name: d for d in DIMENSIONS}
   126→
   127→TIER_WEIGHTS = {
   128→    Tier.AUTO_FIX: 1,
   129→    Tier.QUICK_FIX: 2,
   130→    Tier.JUDGMENT: 3,
   131→    Tier.MAJOR_REFACTOR: 4,
   132→}
   133→CONFIDENCE_WEIGHTS = {Confidence.HIGH: 1.0, Confidence.MEDIUM: 0.7, Confidence.LOW: 0.3}
   134→
   135→# Minimum checks for full dimension weight — below this, weight is dampened
   136→# proportionally. Prevents small-sample dimensions from swinging the overall score.
   137→MIN_SAMPLE = 200
   138→
   139→# Holistic review weight: findings with file="." and detail.holistic=True
   140→# get a 10x weight multiplier for display/priority purposes (issues list,
   141→# remediation engine).  NOT used in score computation — review findings are
   142→# excluded from the detection scoring pipeline (scored via assessments only).
   143→HOLISTIC_MULTIPLIER = 10.0
   144→HOLISTIC_POTENTIAL = 10
   145→
   146→# Budget: subjective dimensions get this fraction of the overall score.
   147→# Mechanical dimensions get the remainder.
   148→SUBJECTIVE_WEIGHT_FRACTION = 0.60
   149→MECHANICAL_WEIGHT_FRACTION = 1.0 - SUBJECTIVE_WEIGHT_FRACTION
   150→
```

```
    33→
    34→def _display_fallback(dim_name: str) -> str:
    35→    words = dim_name.replace("_", " ")
    36→    return words[0].upper() + words[1:] if words else words
    37→
    38→
    39→def _normalize_dimension_key(dim_name: object) -> str:
    40→    if not isinstance(dim_name, str):
    41→        return ""
    42→    return "_".join(dim_name.strip().lower().replace("-", "_").split())
    43→
    44→
    45→def _primary_lang_from_findings(findings: dict) -> str | None:
    46→    counts: dict[str, int] = {}
    47→    for finding in findings.values():
    48→        if not isinstance(finding, dict):
    49→            continue
    50→        raw_lang = finding.get("lang")
    51→        if not isinstance(raw_lang, str) or not raw_lang.strip():
    52→            continue
    53→        key = raw_lang.strip().lower()
    54→        counts[key] = counts.get(key, 0) + 1
    55→    if not counts:
    56→        return None
    57→    return max(counts, key=counts.get)
    58→
    59→
    60→def _dimension_display_name(dim_name: str, *, lang_name: str | None) -> str:
    61→    try:
    62→        # Deferred import to avoid circular: scoring -> _scoring/results/core
    63→        # -> _scoring/subjective/core -> intelligence.review -> state -> scoring
    64→        from desloppify.intelligence.review.dimensions.metadata import (
    65→            dimension_display_name,
    66→        )
    67→
    68→        return str(dimension_display_name(dim_name, lang_name=lang_name))
    69→    except (ImportError, AttributeError, RuntimeError, ValueError, TypeError):
    70→        return DISPLAY_NAMES.get(dim_name, _display_fallback(dim_name))
    71→
    72→
    73→def _dimension_weight(dim_name: str, *, lang_name: str | None) -> float:
    74→    try:
    75→        # Deferred import to avoid circular (see _dimension_display_name).
    76→        from desloppify.intelligence.review.dimensions.metadata import (
    77→            dimension_weight,
    78→        )
    79→
    80→        return float(dimension_weight(dim_name, lang_name=lang_name))
    81→    except (ImportError, AttributeError, RuntimeError, ValueError, TypeError):
    82→        return 1.0
    83→
    84→
    85→def append_subjective_dimensions(
    86→    results: dict,
    87→    findings: dict,
    88→    assessments: dict | None,
    89→    failure_set: frozenset[str],
    90→    allowed_dimensions: set[str] | None = None,
    91→) -> None:

    93→
    94→    Subjective scoring is evidence-first: open review findings for a dimension
    95→    determine pass-rate, while imported assessment scores are retained as
    96→    metadata for transparency.
    97→    """
    98→    # Deferred import to avoid circular (see _dimension_display_name).
    99→    from desloppify.intelligence.review.dimensions.holistic import DIMENSIONS
   100→
```

```
40→def try_dimension_rewrite(pattern: str) -> str | None:
    41→    """If pattern matches a dimension name/key, return a detector scope. Otherwise None."""
    42→    lookup = _build_dimension_lookup()
    43→    lowered = pattern.lower().replace(" ", "_")
    44→    detectors = lookup.get(lowered)
    45→    if not detectors:
    46→        # Try display name form (with spaces)
    47→        detectors = lookup.get(pattern.lower())
    48→    if not detectors:
    49→        return None
    50→    # Return first detector as scope — the work queue will match on detector name.
    51→    # For multi-detector dimensions, we can't pass multiple scopes through the current
    52→    # interface, but most dimensions have one primary detector. Use glob pattern.
    53→    if len(detectors) == 1:
    54→        return detectors[0]
    55→    # For multi-detector dimensions, use wildcard pattern that scope_matches handles
    56→    return None  # Fall through to load_matches_for_dimension
    57→
    58→
    59→def load_matches_for_dimension(
    60→    state: dict,
    61→    pattern: str,
    62→    *,
    63→    status_filter: str,
    64→) -> tuple[list[dict], str | None]:
    65→    """Try to load findings matching a dimension name. Returns (matches, rewritten_pattern)."""
    66→    lookup = _build_dimension_lookup()
    67→    lowered = pattern.lower().replace(" ", "_")
    68→    detectors = lookup.get(lowered) or lookup.get(pattern.lower())
    69→    if not detectors:
    70→        return [], None
    71→
    72→    # Load findings for each detector in this dimension
    73→    all_matches: list[dict] = []
    74→    for detector in detectors:
    75→        matches = load_matches(
    76→            state, scope=detector, status_filter=status_filter, chronic=False
    77→        )
    78→        all_matches.extend(matches)
    79→    # Deduplicate by finding ID
    80→    seen: set[str] = set()
    81→    unique: list[dict] = []
    82→    for item in all_matches:
    83→        fid = item.get("id", "")
    84→        if fid not in seen:
    85→            seen.add(fid)
    86→            unique.append(item)
    87→
    88→    # Find the display name for the dimension
    89→    dim_display = pattern
    90→    for dim in scoring_mod.DIMENSIONS:
    91→        if dim.name.lower() == lowered or dim.name.lower().replace(" ", "_") == lowered:
    92→            dim_display = dim.name
    93→            break
    94→
    95→    return unique, dim_display
```

```
    23→def _load_json_payload_from_path(path: Path) -> dict:
    24→    """Load a JSON payload from *path* and return a dict."""
    25→    try:
    26→        raw = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    27→    except OSError as exc:
    28→        raise RuntimeError(f"Unable to read dimensions payload: {path}") from exc
    29→
    30→    try:
    31→        payload = json.loads(raw)
    32→    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
    33→        raise ValueError(f"Invalid JSON in dimensions payload: {path}") from exc
    34→
    35→    if not isinstance(payload, dict):
    36→        raise ValueError(f"Dimensions payload must be a JSON object: {path}")
    37→    return payload
    38→
    39→
    40→def _lang_payload_path(lang_name: str, filename: str) -> Path:
    41→    """Resolve the per-language review payload path."""
    42→    return _LANG_DIR / lang_name / _LANG_DATA_SUBDIR / filename
    43→
    44→
    45→def _override_filename(filename: str) -> str:
    46→    """Convert ``foo.json`` to ``foo.override.json``."""
    47→    if not filename.endswith(".json"):
    48→        return f"{filename}.override.json"
    49→    return f"{filename[:-5]}.override.json"
    50→
    51→
    52→def _load_json_payload(filename: str) -> dict:
    53→    """Load a JSON payload from the shared language review-data directory."""
    54→    return _load_json_payload_from_path(_DATA_DIR / filename)

    57→def _validate_optional_string_list(value: object, *, context: str) -> list[str]:
    58→    """Validate an optional list of strings (empty allowed)."""
    59→    if value is None:
    60→        return []
    61→    if not isinstance(value, list):
    62→        raise ValueError(f"{context} must be a list of strings")
    63→    out: list[str] = []
    64→    for idx, item in enumerate(value):
    65→        if not isinstance(item, str) or not item.strip():
    66→            raise ValueError(f"{context}[{idx}] must be a non-empty string")
    67→        out.append(item)

    73→    override_payload: dict,

    75→    dims_key: str,

    77→) -> dict:
    78→    """Apply a language override payload to a base dimensions payload."""

    82→    allowed = {
    83→        dims_key,

    88→        "system_prompt",
    89→        "system_prompt_append",
    90→    }
    91→    actual = set(override_payload)
    92→    extra = sorted(actual - allowed)
    93→    if extra:
    94→        raise ValueError(f"{context} has unsupported keys: {extra}")
    95→
    96→    out = copy.deepcopy(base_payload)
    97→
    98→    if dims_key in override_payload:
    99→        out[dims_key] = override_payload[dims_key]
   100→
   101→    dims = list(out.get(dims_key, []))
   102→    for dim in _validate_optional_string_list(
   103→        override_payload.get(f"{dims_key}_append"),
   104→        context=f"{context}.{dims_key}_append",
   105→    ):
   106→        if dim not in dims:
   107→            dims.append(dim)
   108→
   109→    remove_dims = set(
   110→        _validate_optional_string_list(
   111→            override_payload.get(f"{dims_key}_remove"),
   112→            context=f"{context}.{dims_key}_remove",
   113→        )
   114→    )
   115→    if remove_dims:
   116→        dims = [dim for dim in dims if dim not in remove_dims]
   117→    out[dims_key] = dims
   118→
   119→    if "dimension_prompts" in override_payload:
   120→        prompt_overrides = override_payload["dimension_prompts"]
   121→        if not isinstance(prompt_overrides, dict):
   122→            raise ValueError(f"{context}.dimension_prompts must be an object")
   123→        prompts = dict(out.get("dimension_prompts", {}))
   124→        for dim_name, prompt in prompt_overrides.items():
   125→            prompts[dim_name] = prompt
   126→        out["dimension_prompts"] = prompts
   127→
   128→    remove_prompts = _validate_optional_string_list(
   129→        override_payload.get("dimension_prompts_remove"),
   130→        context=f"{context}.dimension_prompts_remove",
   131→    )
   132→    if remove_prompts:
   133→        prompts = dict(out.get("dimension_prompts", {}))
   134→        for dim_name in remove_prompts:
   135→            prompts.pop(dim_name, None)

   137→
   138→    if "system_prompt" in override_payload:
   139→        out["system_prompt"] = override_payload["system_prompt"]

   144→        current = out.get("system_prompt", "")
   145→        sep = "\n\n" if current and suffix else ""
   146→        out["system_prompt"] = f"{current}{sep}{suffix}"
   147→
   148→    return out
```

```
   101→def _build_dimensions() -> list[Dimension]:

   103→
   104→    Each unique (dimension, tier) pair becomes a Dimension, with its detectors

   118→    return [

   120→        for name, tier in dim_tiers.items()
   121→    ]
```

```
1→"""Validation/parsing helpers for review dimensions payloads."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.core._internal.text_utils import is_numeric
     6→
     7→_PROMPT_FIELDS = ("description", "look_for", "skip")
     8→_PROMPT_OPTIONAL_FIELDS = ("meta",)

    10→
    11→
    12→def validate_payload_keys(
    13→    payload: dict,
    14→    *,
    15→    required: set[str],
    16→    context: str,
    17→    optional: set[str] | None = None,
    18→) -> None:
    19→    """Validate payload has required keys and no unknown keys."""
    20→    optional_keys = optional or set()
    21→    allowed = set(required) | set(optional_keys)
    22→    actual = set(payload)
    23→    missing = sorted(required - actual)
    24→    extra = sorted(actual - allowed)
    25→    if missing or extra:
    26→        raise ValueError(
    27→            f"{context} keys invalid (missing={missing or []}, extra={extra or []})"
    28→        )
    29→
    30→
    31→def validate_string_list(value: object, *, context: str) -> list[str]:
    32→    """Validate a non-empty list of non-empty strings."""
    33→    if not isinstance(value, list) or not value:
    34→        raise ValueError(f"{context} must be a non-empty list of strings")
    35→
    36→    out: list[str] = []
    37→    for idx, item in enumerate(value):
    38→        if not isinstance(item, str) or not item.strip():
    39→            raise ValueError(f"{context}[{idx}] must be a non-empty string")
    40→        out.append(item)
    41→    return out
    42→
    43→
    44→def validate_prompt_meta(value: object, *, context: str) -> dict[str, object]:
    45→    """Validate optional prompt-level metadata object."""
    46→    if value is None:
    47→        return {}
    48→    if not isinstance(value, dict):
    49→        raise ValueError(f"{context} must be an object")
    50→
    51→    validate_payload_keys(
    52→        value,
    53→        required=set(),
    54→        optional={"enabled_by_default", "display_name", "weight", "reset_on_scan"},
    55→        context=context,
    56→    )
    57→
    58→    out: dict[str, object] = {}
    59→
    60→    enabled = value.get("enabled_by_default")
    61→    if enabled is not None:
    62→        if not isinstance(enabled, bool):
    63→            raise ValueError(f"{context}.enabled_by_default must be a boolean")
    64→        out["enabled_by_default"] = enabled
    65→
    66→    display_name = value.get("display_name")
    67→    if display_name is not None:
    68→        if not isinstance(display_name, str) or not display_name.strip():
    69→            raise ValueError(f"{context}.display_name must be a non-empty string")
    70→        out["display_name"] = display_name.strip()
    71→
    72→    weight = value.get("weight")
    73→    if weight is not None:
    74→        if not is_numeric(weight):
    75→            raise ValueError(f"{context}.weight must be a number")
    76→        if float(weight) < 0:
    77→            raise ValueError(f"{context}.weight must be >= 0")
    78→        out["weight"] = float(weight)
    79→
    80→    reset_on_scan = value.get("reset_on_scan")
    81→    if reset_on_scan is not None:
    82→        if not isinstance(reset_on_scan, bool):
    83→            raise ValueError(f"{context}.reset_on_scan must be a boolean")
    84→        out["reset_on_scan"] = reset_on_scan
    85→
    86→    return out
    87→
    88→
    89→def validate_dimension_prompts(
    90→    value: object, *, context: str
    91→) -> dict[str, dict[str, object]]:
    92→    """Validate the dimension prompt map schema."""
    93→    if not isinstance(value, dict) or not value:
    94→        raise ValueError(f"{context} must be a non-empty object")
    95→

   116→            )

   121→                entry.get("look_for"),

   136→def validate_dimensions_list(value: object, *, context: str) -> list[str]:
   137→    """Validate dimension names list."""
   138→    dims = validate_string_list(value, context=context)

   140→    duplicates: list[str] = []
   141→    for dim in dims:
   142→        if dim in seen:
   143→            duplicates.append(dim)
   144→        seen.add(dim)
   145→    if duplicates:
   146→        raise ValueError(
   147→            f"{context} contains duplicate entries: {sorted(set(duplicates))}"
   148→        )
   149→    return dims
   150→

   155→        raise ValueError(
   156→            f"{context} must be a non-empty string with at least {_MIN_SYSTEM_PROMPT_LEN} chars"
   157→        )

   181→    prompts = validate_dimension_prompts(

   189→
   190→    for dim_name, entry in prompts.items():
   191→        meta = entry.get("meta")
   192→        if isinstance(meta, dict) and meta.get("enabled_by_default") is True:
```

## Summary of Findings: Subjective vs Mechanical Dimension Classification

Based on my thorough investigation of the codebase, here's how the distinction between subjective and mechanical dimensions is structured:

### 1. **Core Definition: The Dimension Dataclass**

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_scoring/policy/core.py` (lines 15-19)

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Dimension:
    name: str
    tier: int
    detectors: list[str]
```

The `Dimension` type itself is **agnostic** about subjective vs mechanical. The distinction is determined indirectly through:
- Which **detectors** are in the `detectors` list
- Whether `"subjective_assessment"` appears in that list

### 2. **How Dimensions Get Their Detectors**

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_scoring/policy/core.py` (lines 101-124)

The `_build_dimensions()` function derives dimensions from `DETECTOR_SCORING_POLICIES` (a dict mapping detector names to `DetectorScoringPolicy` objects). Each policy specifies:
- The detector name
- Which dimension it belongs to (or `None` if not scored mechanically)
- Tier level

**Key insight:** Only mechanical detectors are organized into `DIMENSIONS`. Subjective detectors like `"subjective_assessment"` and `"subjective_review"` are added **post-hoc** via `append_subjective_dimensions()` in the scoring results phase.

### 3. **Canonical Classification Pattern (Ad-Hoc, Not Centralized)**

**The concept of "is this dimension subjective?" does NOT have a single canonical home.** Instead, it's re-derived in multiple places using the same pattern:

```python
is_subjective = "subjective_assessment" in data.get("detectors", {})
```

**Places where this check occurs:**

| File | Function/Context | Line Pattern |
|------|-----------------|---------|
| `engine/_scoring/results/core.py` | `compute_health_breakdown()` | Line 184 |
| `engine/_scoring/results/core.py` | `compute_score_bundle()` | Line 325 |
| `engine/_state/scoring.py` | Score splitting logic | Line 129 |
| `app/output/scorecard_parts/dimensions.py` | `limit_scorecard_dimensions()` | Line 141, 146 |
| `app/output/scorecard_parts/dimensions.py` | `is_unassessed_subjective_placeholder()` | Line 50 |
| `app/commands/show/cmd.py` | Dimension lookup | Lines 115-117 |
| `engine/planning/scorecard_projection.py` | `scorecard_subjective_entries()` | Line 86 |
| `engine/_work_queue/helpers.py` | `is_subjective_finding()` | Lines 36-42 |

### 4. **Mechanical vs Subjective Weight Dictionaries**

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_scoring/policy/core.py` (lines 153-178)

```python
MECHANICAL_DIMENSION_WEIGHTS: dict[str, float] = {
    "file health": 2.0,
    "code quality": 1.0,
    "duplication": 1.0,
    "test health": 1.0,
    "security": 1.0,
}

SUBJECTIVE_DIMENSION_WEIGHTS: dict[str, float] = {
    "high elegance": 22.0,
    "mid elegance": 22.0,
    "low elegance": 12.0,
    "contracts": 12.0,
    "type safety": 12.0,
    "abstraction fit": 8.0,
    "logic clarity": 6.0,

}
```

**These are the primary source of truth for which dimensions exist in each pool.** They're used by:
- `_mechanical_dimension_weight()` and `_subjective_dimension_weight()` in `engine/_scoring/results/core.py`
- Scoring aggregation in `compute_health_breakdown()`

### 5. **Subjective Dimension Display Names**

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_scoring/subjective/core.py` (lines 8-32)

```python
DISPLAY_NAMES: dict[str, str] = {
    "cross_module_architecture": "Cross-module arch",
    "initialization_coupling": "Init coupling",
    "convention_outlier": "Convention drift",
    # ... and 22 more subjective dimensions
    "contract_coherence": "Contracts",
}
```

**Purpose:** Maps canonical dimension keys to human-readable names for scoring output. Used by:
- `_dimension_display_name()` in the same file
- `engine/planning/scorecard_projection.py` for CLI key resolution
- `app/commands/show/cmd.py` for dimension name matching (lines 120-126)

**Important:** `DISPLAY_NAMES` is **only for subjective dimensions**. Mechanical dimensions use their plain `Dimension.name` values directly.

### 6. **Holistic/Per-File Review Dimensions**

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/dimensions/holistic.py` and JSON data files

These are defined in a separate payload system with metadata:
- `default_dimensions`: list of which ones are enabled by default
- `dimension_prompts`: prompt definitions with optional `meta.display_name`, `meta.weight`, `meta.reset_on_scan`

Per-language overrides can be defined in `languages/{lang_name}/review_data/dimensions.override.json`.

### 7. **How `scorecard_subjective_entries()` Identifies Subjective Dimensions**

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/planning/scorecard_projection.py` (lines 70-122)

```python
def scorecard_subjective_entries(state: dict, *, dim_scores: dict | None = None) -> list[dict]:
    rows = scorecard_dimension_rows(state, dim_scores=dim_scores)
    assessments = state.get("subjective_assessments") or {}
    subjective_display_names = {display.lower() for display in DISPLAY_NAMES.values()}
    subjective_display_names.update({"elegance", "elegance (combined)"})

    entries: list[dict] = []
    for name, data in rows:
        detectors = data.get("detectors", {})
        lowered_name = str(name).strip().lower()
        is_subjective = (
            "subjective_assessment" in detectors  # PRIMARY CHECK
            or lowered_name in subjective_display_names  # FALLBACK
        )
```

Uses **two heuristics** (in order of precedence):
1. Check if `"subjective_assessment"` is in the detectors dict (most reliable)
2. Check if the display name matches a known subjective display name (fallback for edge cases)

### 8. **Helper: Finding if an Item is Subjective**

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/helpers.py` (lines 36-42)

```python
def is_subjective_finding(item: dict) -> bool:
    detector = item.get("detector")
    if detector in {"subjective_assessment"}:
        return True
    if detector == "holistic_review":
        return True
    return False
```

Classifies **findings/items** (not dimensions) as subjective based on their detector. This is a **finding-level** check, not a dimension-level check.

---

## Key Architectural Insights

### Missing Centralization

There is **no single canonical function** `is_subjective_dimension(dimension_data: dict) -> bool`. Instead:
- The check `"subjective_assessment" in data.get("detectors", {})` is inlined/re-derived everywhere
- Two weight dicts (`MECHANICAL_DIMENSION_WEIGHTS` and `SUBJECTIVE_DIMENSION_WEIGHTS`) are the closest to a canonical source of truth
- `DISPLAY_NAMES` is subjective-only, not a comprehensive registry

### Recommendation for Centralization

You could create a single utility function in `engine/_scoring/results/core.py` or `engine/_scoring/policy/core.py`:

```python
def is_dimension_subjective(dimension_data: dict) -> bool:
    """Return True if a dimension is subjective (has subjective_assessment detector)."""
    return "subjective_assessment" in dimension_data.get("detectors", {})
```

Then import and use it everywhere instead of the inlined check. This would make it explicit and easier to refactor if the classification rule ever changes.

### Current Design Rationale

The current ad-hoc approach works because:
1. The classification rule is **simple and stable** (presence of a specific detector)
2. It's used in **hot paths** where inlining avoids import overhead
3. Each site has sufficient context to make the check locally
4. The weight dicts serve as the actual registry of which dimensions are in each pool

---

**Mechanical dimensions (from MECHANICAL_DIMENSION_WEIGHTS):**
- File health, Code quality, Duplication, Test health, Security

## Structural Analysis: "No Matches" Fallback in `cmd_show` (Lines 99-213)

Based on my thorough examination of the code, here's the complete structural assessment:

---

### 1. **Full Branch Map: All Distinct Outcomes in the "No Matches" Path**

The fallback block (lines 99-213 in `cmd.py`) handles **7 distinct execution paths**:

| Line | Condition | Outcome |
|------|-----------|---------|
| 101-106 | `load_matches_for_dimension()` returns findings | **Path A:** Reuse dimension matches and display them normally (continue to line 215) |
| 107-138 | Pattern is mechanical dimension WITH subjective detectors | **Path B:** Check subjective dimension logic (fall through to check) |
| 128-165 | Pattern matches subjective dimension | **Path C:** Display dimension score + health stats + subjective dashboard; return early |
| 168-187 | Pattern is literal "subjective" or "subjective_review" | **Path D:** Show subjective dashboard with empty findings; return early |
| 189-213 | Generic "no matches" with detector hint | **Path E:** Generic error message + detector list hint; return early |

**Summary:**
- **3 early returns** (C, D, E) — each prints output and exits
- **1 fallthrough** (A) — reuses dimension matches, merges into main flow
- **1 conditional check** (B) — guards against incorrectly flagging multi-detector dimensions as subjective

---

### 2. **Distinct Concerns Mixed in Fallback Block**

**5 semi-orthogonal concerns are interleaved:**

| Concern | Lines | Purpose |
|---------|-------|---------|
| **Dimension Resolution** | 101-106 | Detect if pattern is dimension name; load all detectors' findings |
| **Subjective Classification** | 107-138 | Identify if dimension is subjective via DIMENSIONS + DISPLAY_NAMES + dimension_scores |
| **Score Display** | 139-145 | Render health % and strict % for subjective dimensions |
| **Finding Retrieval** | 150-155 | Query review findings tagged with dimension |
| **UI Rendering** | 146-165, 172-213 | Print different messages for different context (subjective vs. generic no-match) |

**Why this is a problem:**
- Each concern requires its own conditional logic
- Subjective dimension detection (lines 108-138) uses 3 different lookups (DIMENSIONS, DISPLAY_NAMES, state.dimension_scores)
- Score retrieval is mixed with UI presentation
- Lines 150-155 iterate over *all findings in state* to match by dimension, which is inefficient

---

### 3. **Load-Matches-For-Dimension Already Exists — Why Redundant Logic?**

`load_matches_for_dimension` in `scope.py` (lines 59-95) **already handles detector-based finding lookup**:

```python
def load_matches_for_dimension(state, pattern, *, status_filter):
    lookup = _build_dimension_lookup()      # ← Maps dim name → detectors
    detectors = lookup.get(lowered) or lookup.get(pattern.lower())
    if not detectors:
        return [], None
    
    all_matches: list[dict] = []
    for detector in detectors:             # ← Loads from each detector
        matches = load_matches(state, scope=detector, status_filter=status_filter, ...)
        all_matches.extend(matches)
    # ...deduplicate...
    return unique, dim_display
```

**But `cmd.py` lines 101-106 just call this and bail on first non-empty result.** Lines 108-138 then **re-implement dimension lookup a second time** to check for subjective detectors. This is **duplication + inconsistency:**

- **Line 112**: Rebuilds dimension lookup via `DIMENSIONS` directly
- **Line 120**: Checks DISPLAY_NAMES (separate lookup)
- **Line 123-126**: Checks state.dimension_scores directly
- **Line 151-155**: Manually queries findings for dimension match

All of this *could* be done in `load_matches_for_dimension` or a sibling function.

---

### 4. **What Does `resolve_show_scope` Do? Can It Be Extended?**

`resolve_show_scope` (lines 107-125 in `scope.py`):

```python
def resolve_show_scope(args) -> tuple[bool, str | None, str, str | None]:
    # Returns: (ok, pattern, status_filter, scope)
    # scope = pattern (no translation)
    return True, pattern, status_filter, pattern
```

**Current behavior:**
- Takes raw pattern from args
- Returns it unchanged as both `pattern` and `scope`
- Does **zero pre-processing** — no dimension name rewriting

**Could it be extended?**

Yes, absolutely. It could call `try_dimension_rewrite(pattern)` here to convert dimension names to detector scopes *before* calling `load_matches`. This would collapse the "no matches" fallback significantly:

```python
def resolve_show_scope(args) -> tuple[bool, str | None, str, str | None]:
    pattern = args.pattern
    # ← NEW: Try rewriting dimension → detector(s)
    rewritten_scope = try_dimension_rewrite(pattern)
    if rewritten_scope:
        scope = rewritten_scope
    else:
        scope = pattern  # Leave as-is for file/detector matching
    return True, pattern, status_filter, scope
```

**Problem:** `try_dimension_rewrite` returns `None` for **multi-detector dimensions**, which is why the fallback exists.

---

### 5. **The `try_dimension_rewrite` Limitation: Root Cause of Fallback**

Here's the critical code (lines 40-56 in `scope.py`):

```python
def try_dimension_rewrite(pattern: str) -> str | None:
    """If pattern matches a dimension name/key, return a detector scope. Otherwise None."""
    lookup = _build_dimension_lookup()
    detectors = lookup.get(lowered)
    if not detectors:
        return None
    
    if len(detectors) == 1:
        return detectors[0]  # ← Single detector: can return it
    # For multi-detector dimensions, use wildcard pattern...
    return None  # ← Multi-detector: GIVES UP, falls through
```

**The problem:**

- **Single-detector dimensions** (e.g., "Code quality" has 15+ detectors) → Can't be passed through scope
- **Multi-detector dimensions** like "Security" (detectors: `security`, `cycles`) → Returns None

This is the **architectural gap** that forces the fallback logic.

---

### 6. **How Scope Matching Works: What Can Scope Match?**

From `scope_matches` in `helpers.py` (lines 63-100):

```python
def scope_matches(item: dict, scope: str | None) -> bool:
    if not scope:
        return True  # ← No scope = all items match
    
    item_id = item.get("id", "")
    detector = item.get("detector", "")
    filepath = item.get("file", "")
    summary = item.get("summary", "")
    dimension = detail_dict(item).get("dimension_name", "")
    kind = item.get("kind", "")
    
    if "*" in scope:  # ← Glob patterns
        return any(fnmatch(candidate, scope) for candidate in (...))
    
    if "::" in scope:  # ← Finding ID prefix
        return item_id.startswith(scope)
    
    lowered = scope.lower()
    if kind == "subjective_dimension":  # ← Can match subjective_dimension items
        return lowered in item_id.lower() or lowered in dimension.lower() or lowered in summary.lower()
    
    # Hash suffix match
    if len(lowered) >= 8 and re.fullmatch(r"[0-9a-f]+", lowered):
        return item_id.lower().endswith("::" + lowered)
    
    return (
        detector == scope          # ← Direct detector name match
        or filepath == scope       # ← File path match
        or filepath.startswith(scope.rstrip("/") + "/")  # ← Directory match
    )
```

**Key insight:** `scope_matches` does **NOT** match dimension names directly. It only matches:
- Detector names (exact string)
- File paths (exact or directory prefix)
- Finding IDs (prefix or hash suffix)
- Glob patterns
- Subjective dimension items (by `kind == "subjective_dimension"`)

**Dimension names are never matched by `scope_matches` itself.**

This means:
- `load_matches(scope="security")` — works (detector name)
- `load_matches(scope="Security")` — **FAILS** (not a detector)
- `load_matches(scope="security|cycles")` — **FAILS** (no multi-detector syntax)

---

### 7. **Conclusion: Is the Messiness Due to Gaps or Accumulated Complexity?**

**Answer: BOTH.**

#### **Architectural Gaps:**

1. **`scope_matches` doesn't understand dimension names**
   - It only handles detector names, file paths, and IDs
   - Dimension-to-detector translation must happen *before* passing to `load_matches`
   - But `try_dimension_rewrite` bails on multi-detector dimensions

2. **`try_dimension_rewrite` can't handle multi-detector dimensions**
   - Returns `None` for "Security" (which has `security` + `cycles` detectors)
   - Returns `None` for "Code quality" (which has 15+ detectors)
   - This forces a fallback to `load_matches_for_dimension`, which is called *only if first call to `load_matches` fails*

3. **No single "dimension aware" scope mechanism**
   - `scope.py` has *two* separate functions: `try_dimension_rewrite` and `load_matches_for_dimension`
   - They duplicate the dimension lookup logic
   - One is used in early scope resolution (missing), the other is only used as a fallback in `cmd.py`

#### **Accumulated Complexity:**

1. **Subjective dimension detection logic scattered across 3 places:**
   - Line 112: `DIMENSIONS` lookup (mechanical check)
   - Line 120: `DISPLAY_NAMES` lookup (subjective display names)
   - Line 123-126: `state.dimension_scores` direct lookup (runtime state)
   - Each uses different normalization (lowered, underscore replacement)

2. **Finding retrieval for subjective dimensions (lines 150-155) is O(n)**
   - Iterates *all* state findings to find matches by dimension key
   - Should be pre-computed or cached

3. **UI rendering decisions interleaved with data lookup**
   - Score display (139-145) happens in fallback block
   - Subjective dashboard decision (167-187) happens in fallback block
   - Generic error message (189-213) happens in fallback block
   - Makes testing and reuse harder

---

### **Root Cause Summary**

The messy fallback exists because:

1. **Design gap:** `resolve_show_scope` doesn't try dimension translation → `load_matches` gets raw pattern → fails for dimension names → forces fallback
2. **Implementation gap:** `try_dimension_rewrite` can't handle multi-detector dimensions → returns `None` → fallback must call `load_matches_for_dimension` instead
3. **Complexity accumulation:** Subjective dimension logic (classification, score lookup, finding matching) is split between scope resolution and cmd.py fallback

The gaps are **fixable with refactoring**, but the current architecture makes dimension handling a special case rather than a first-class concept in the pipeline.

```
1→"""Scope and queue helpers for show command selection."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify import scoring as scoring_mod
     6→from desloppify import state as state_mod

    13→
    14→def _build_dimension_lookup() -> dict[str, list[str]]:
    15→    """Build a map from dimension name/key (lowered) to detector names."""
    16→    lookup: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
    17→    for dim in scoring_mod.DIMENSIONS:
    18→        detectors = list(dim.detectors) if hasattr(dim, "detectors") else []
    19→        lookup[dim.name.lower()] = detectors
    20→        # Also index by underscore key: "file_health" -> detectors
    21→        key = dim.name.lower().replace(" ", "_")
    22→        if key not in lookup:
    23→            lookup[key] = detectors
    24→    # Also add DISPLAY_NAMES reverse lookup (e.g. "abstraction_fit" -> "Abstraction Fit")
    25→    for key, display in scoring_mod.DISPLAY_NAMES.items():
    26→        normalized_key = key.lower().replace(" ", "_")
    27→        normalized_display = display.lower()
    28→        # Find which dimension this belongs to via get_dimension_for_detector or direct name match
    29→        for dim in scoring_mod.DIMENSIONS:
    30→            dim_lower = dim.name.lower()
    31→            if normalized_display == dim_lower or normalized_key == dim_lower.replace(" ", "_"):
    32→                detectors = list(dim.detectors) if hasattr(dim, "detectors") else []
    33→                if normalized_key not in lookup:
    34→                    lookup[normalized_key] = detectors
    35→                if normalized_display not in lookup:
    36→                    lookup[normalized_display] = detectors
    37→    return lookup
    38→
    39→
    40→def try_dimension_rewrite(pattern: str) -> str | None:
    41→    """If pattern matches a dimension name/key, return a detector scope. Otherwise None."""
    42→    lookup = _build_dimension_lookup()
    43→    lowered = pattern.lower().replace(" ", "_")
    44→    detectors = lookup.get(lowered)
    45→    if not detectors:
    46→        # Try display name form (with spaces)
    47→        detectors = lookup.get(pattern.lower())
    48→    if not detectors:
    49→        return None
    50→    # Return first detector as scope — the work queue will match on detector name.
    51→    # For multi-detector dimensions, we can't pass multiple scopes through the current
    52→    # interface, but most dimensions have one primary detector. Use glob pattern.
    53→    if len(detectors) == 1:
    54→        return detectors[0]
    55→    # For multi-detector dimensions, use wildcard pattern that scope_matches handles
    56→    return None  # Fall through to load_matches_for_dimension
    57→
    58→
    59→def load_matches_for_dimension(
    60→    state: dict,
    61→    pattern: str,
    62→    *,
    63→    status_filter: str,
    64→) -> tuple[list[dict], str | None]:
    65→    """Try to load findings matching a dimension name. Returns (matches, rewritten_pattern)."""
    66→    lookup = _build_dimension_lookup()
    67→    lowered = pattern.lower().replace(" ", "_")
    68→    detectors = lookup.get(lowered) or lookup.get(pattern.lower())
    69→    if not detectors:
    70→        return [], None
    71→
    72→    # Load findings for each detector in this dimension
    73→    all_matches: list[dict] = []
    74→    for detector in detectors:
    75→        matches = load_matches(
    76→            state, scope=detector, status_filter=status_filter, chronic=False
    77→        )
    78→        all_matches.extend(matches)
    79→    # Deduplicate by finding ID
    80→    seen: set[str] = set()
    81→    unique: list[dict] = []
    82→    for item in all_matches:
    83→        fid = item.get("id", "")
    84→        if fid not in seen:
    85→            seen.add(fid)
    86→            unique.append(item)
    87→
    88→    # Find the display name for the dimension
    89→    dim_display = pattern
    90→    for dim in scoring_mod.DIMENSIONS:
    91→        if dim.name.lower() == lowered or dim.name.lower().replace(" ", "_") == lowered:
    92→            dim_display = dim.name
    93→            break
    94→
    95→    return unique, dim_display
    96→
    97→
    98→def _detector_names_hint() -> str:
    99→    """Return a compact list of detector names for the help message."""
   100→    from desloppify.core import registry as registry_mod
   101→    names = getattr(registry_mod, "DISPLAY_ORDER", [])
   102→    if names:
   103→        return ", ".join(names[:10]) + (", ..." if len(names) > 10 else "")
   104→    return "smells, structural, security, review, ..."
   105→
   106→
   107→def resolve_show_scope(args) -> tuple[bool, str | None, str, str | None]:
   108→    """Resolve scope/pattern/status for a show invocation."""
   109→    chronic = getattr(args, "chronic", False)
   110→    pattern = args.pattern
   111→    status_filter = "open" if chronic else getattr(args, "status", "open")
   112→    if chronic:
   113→        scope = pattern
   114→        pattern = pattern or "<chronic>"
   115→        return True, pattern, status_filter, scope
   116→    if not pattern:
   117→        print(
   118→            colorize(
   119→                "Pattern required (or use --chronic). Try: desloppify show --help",
   120→                "yellow",
   121→            )
   122→        )
   123→        return False, None, status_filter, ""
   124→    return True, pattern, status_filter, pattern
   125→
   126→
   127→def load_matches(
   128→    state: dict,
   129→    *,
   130→    scope: str | None,
   131→    status_filter: str,
   132→    chronic: bool,
   133→) -> list[dict]:
   134→    """Load matching findings from the ranked queue."""
   135→    queue = build_work_queue(
   136→        state,
   137→        options=QueueBuildOptions(
   138→            count=None,
   139→            scan_path=state.get("scan_path"),
   140→            scope=scope,
   141→            status=status_filter,
   142→            include_subjective=False,
   143→            chronic=chronic,
   144→            no_tier_fallback=True,
   145→        ),
   146→    )
   147→    return [item for item in queue.get("items", []) if item.get("kind") == "finding"]
   148→
   149→
   150→def resolve_noise(config: dict, matches: list[dict]):
   151→    """Apply detector/global noise budget to show matches."""
   152→    noise_budget, global_noise_budget, budget_warning = (
   153→        state_mod.resolve_finding_noise_settings(config)
   154→    )
   155→    surfaced_matches, hidden_by_detector = state_mod.apply_finding_noise_budget(
   156→        matches,
   157→        budget=noise_budget,
   158→        global_budget=global_noise_budget,
   159→    )
   160→    return (
   161→        surfaced_matches,
   162→        hidden_by_detector,
   163→        noise_budget,
   164→        global_noise_budget,
   165→        budget_warning,
   166→    )
   167→
   168→
   169→__all__ = [

   171→    "load_matches",
   172→    "load_matches_for_dimension",
   173→    "resolve_noise",
   174→    "resolve_show_scope",
   175→]
```

```
   239→            str(key).strip().lower()
   240→            for key in entry.get("cli_keys", [])
   241→            if str(key).strip()
   242→        ]
   243→        aliases.update(cli_keys)
   244→        aliases.update(slugify(key) for key in cli_keys)
   245→        open_review = sum(review_open_by_dim.get(alias, 0) for alias in aliases)
   246→        is_unassessed = bool(entry.get("placeholder")) or (
   247→            name in unassessed_dims
   248→            or (strict_val <= 0.0 and int(entry.get("issues", 0)) == 0)
   249→        )
   250→        is_stale = bool(entry.get("stale"))
   251→        if is_unassessed:
   252→            primary_command = "desloppify review --prepare"
   253→        elif is_stale:
   254→            if cli_keys:
   255→                primary_command = (
   256→                    "desloppify review --prepare --dimensions " + ",".join(cli_keys)
   257→                )
   258→            else:
   259→                primary_command = "desloppify review --prepare"
   260→        else:
   261→            if open_review > 0:
   262→                primary_command = "desloppify show review --status open"
   263→            elif cli_keys:
   264→                primary_command = (
   265→                    "desloppify review --prepare --dimensions " + ",".join(cli_keys)
   266→                )
   267→            else:
   268→                primary_command = "desloppify review --prepare"
   269→        stale_tag = " [stale — re-review]" if is_stale else ""
   270→        summary = f"Subjective dimension below target: {name} ({strict_val:.1f}%){stale_tag}"
   271→        items.append(
   272→            {
   273→                "id": f"subjective::{slugify(dim_key)}",
   274→                "detector": "subjective_assessment",
   275→                "file": ".",
   276→                "tier": 4,
   277→                "effective_tier": 4,
   278→                "confidence": "medium",
   279→                "summary": summary,
   280→                "detail": {
   281→                    "dimension_name": name,
   282→                    "dimension": dim_key,
   283→                    "issues": int(entry.get("issues", 0)),
   284→                    "strict_score": strict_val,
   285→                    "open_review_findings": open_review,
   286→                    "cli_keys": cli_keys,
   287→                },
   288→                "status": "open",
   289→                "kind": "subjective_dimension",
   290→                "primary_command": primary_command,
   291→            }
   292→        )
   293→    return items
   294→
   295→
```
